core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:739: Requests for depth, thoroughness, research, investigation, or detailed codebase analysis do not count as permission to spawn.{agent_role_usage_hint}
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:740: 
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:741: ### When to delegate vs. do the subtask yourself
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:742: - First, quickly analyze the overall user task and form a succinct high-level plan. Identify which tasks are immediate blockers on the critical path, and which tasks are sidecar tasks that are needed but can run in parallel without blocking the next local step. As part of that plan, explicitly decide what immediate task you should do locally right now. Do this planning step before delegating to agents so you do not hand off the immediate blocking task to a submodel and then waste time waiting on it.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:743: - Use a subagent when a subtask is easy enough for it to handle and can run in parallel with your local work. Prefer delegating concrete, bounded sidecar tasks that materially advance the main task without blocking your immediate next local step.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:744: - Do not delegate urgent blocking work when your immediate next step depends on that result. If the very next action is blocked on that task, the main rollout should usually do it locally to keep the critical path moving.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:745: - Keep work local when the subtask is too difficult to delegate well and when it is tightly coupled, urgent, or likely to block your immediate next step.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:746: 
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:747: ### Designing delegated subtasks
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:748: - Subtasks must be concrete, well-defined, and self-contained.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:749: - Delegated subtasks must materially advance the main task.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:750: - Do not duplicate work between the main rollout and delegated subtasks.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:751: - Avoid issuing multiple delegate calls on the same unresolved thread unless the new delegated task is genuinely different and necessary.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:752: - Narrow the delegated ask to the concrete output you need next.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:753: - For coding tasks, prefer delegating concrete code-change worker subtasks over read-only explorer analysis when the subagent can make a bounded patch in a clear write scope.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:754: - When delegating coding work, instruct the submodel to edit files directly in its forked workspace and list the file paths it changed in the final answer.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:755: - For code-edit subtasks, decompose work so each delegated task has a disjoint write set.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:756: 
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:757: ### After you delegate
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:758: - Call wait_agent very sparingly. Only call wait_agent when you need the result immediately for the next critical-path step and you are blocked until it returns.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:759: - Do not redo delegated subagent tasks yourself; focus on integrating results or tackling non-overlapping work.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:760: - While the subagent is running in the background, do meaningful non-overlapping work immediately.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:761: - Do not repeatedly wait by reflex.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:762: - When a delegated coding task returns, quickly review the uploaded changes, then integrate or refine them.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:763: 
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:764: ### Parallel delegation patterns
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:765: - Run multiple independent information-seeking subtasks in parallel when you have distinct questions that can be answered independently.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:766: - Split implementation into disjoint codebase slices and spawn multiple agents for them in parallel when the write scopes do not overlap.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:767: - Delegate verification only when it can run in parallel with ongoing implementation and is likely to catch a concrete risk before final integration.
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:768: - The key is to find opportunities to spawn multiple independent subtasks in parallel within the same round, while ensuring each subtask is well-defined, self-contained, and materially advances the main task."#
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:769:     )
core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs:770: }

core/src/tools/handlers/shell_spec.rs:332: fn windows_shell_guidance() -> &'static str {
core/src/tools/handlers/shell_spec.rs:333:     r#"Windows safety rules:
core/src/tools/handlers/shell_spec.rs:334: - Do not compose destructive filesystem commands across shells. Do not enumerate paths in PowerShell and then pass them to `cmd /c`, batch builtins, or another shell for deletion or moving. Use one shell end-to-end, prefer native PowerShell cmdlets such as `Remove-Item` / `Move-Item` with `-LiteralPath`, and avoid string-built shell commands for file operations.
core/src/tools/handlers/shell_spec.rs:335: - Before any recursive delete or move on Windows, verify the resolved absolute target paths stay within the intended workspace or explicitly named target directory. Never issue a recursive delete or move against a computed path if the final target has not been checked.
core/src/tools/handlers/shell_spec.rs:336: - When using `Start-Process` to launch a background helper or service, pass `-WindowStyle Hidden` unless the user explicitly asked for a visible interactive window. Use visible windows only for interactive tools the user needs to see or control."#
core/src/tools/handlers/shell_spec.rs:337: }

ext/image-generation/imagegen_description.md:2: 
ext/image-generation/imagegen_description.md:3: - The user requests an image based on a scene description, such as a diagram, portrait, comic, meme, or any other visual.
ext/image-generation/imagegen_description.md:4: - The user wants to modify an attached or previously generated image with specific changes, including adding or removing elements, altering colors, improving quality/resolution, or transforming the style (e.g., cartoon, oil painting).
ext/image-generation/imagegen_description.md:5: 
ext/image-generation/imagegen_description.md:6: Guidelines:
ext/image-generation/imagegen_description.md:7: - imagegen needs a few minutes to finish. In code-mode, use the first-line @exec directive to give the initial call 120 seconds and the same yield for any waits that follow. Once it finishes, return the image with generatedImage(result).
ext/image-generation/imagegen_description.md:8: - Avoid printing the full result or its base64 image data with `text()` or `notify()`; print only small metadata when needed.
ext/image-generation/imagegen_description.md:9: - Set `transparent_background` to true when the request calls for a transparent background, including background removal or a cutout; set it to false otherwise. For edits, preserve existing transparency unless the user asks to change it.
ext/image-generation/imagegen_description.md:10: - Omit both `referenced_image_paths` and `num_last_images_to_include` when generating a brand new image.
ext/image-generation/imagegen_description.md:11: - For edits, use `referenced_image_paths` when every target image has a local file path.
ext/image-generation/imagegen_description.md:12: - If you have not seen a local image yet, use `view_image` to inspect it before editing.
ext/image-generation/imagegen_description.md:13: - Use `num_last_images_to_include` only when at least one target image has no local file path.
ext/image-generation/imagegen_description.md:14: - Set `num_last_images_to_include` to the smallest number of recent conversation images that includes every target image, up to 5.
ext/image-generation/imagegen_description.md:15: - Never provide both `referenced_image_paths` and `num_last_images_to_include`.
ext/image-generation/imagegen_description.md:16: - If neither mechanism can include every target image, ask the user to attach the missing images again.
ext/image-generation/imagegen_description.md:17: - Directly generate the image without reconfirmation or clarification unless required images must be attached again.
ext/image-generation/imagegen_description.md:18: - Always use this tool for image editing unless the user explicitly requests otherwise. Do not use the `python` tool for image editing unless specifically instructed.

ext/skills/src/tools/read.rs:423:     }

ext/skills/src/tools/read.rs:425:     let mut lower = start;

ext/skills/src/tools/read.rs:428:     while lower < upper {

ext/skills/src/tools/read.rs:437:         }
ext/skills/src/tools/read.rs:438:     }
ext/skills/src/tools/read.rs:439:     best.ok_or_else(|| {

===== ext/web-search/web_run_description.md =====

ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:7: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:8: Examples of different commands available in this tool:
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:9: * `search_query`: {"search_query": [{"q": "What is the capital of France?"}, {"q": "What is the capital of belgium?"}]}. Searches the internet for a given query (and optionally with a domain or recency filter)
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:10: * `image_query`: {"image_query":[{"q": "waterfalls"}]}.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:11: * `open`: {"open": [{"ref_id": "turn0search0"}, {"ref_id": "https://www.openai.com", "lineno": 120}]}
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:12: * `click`: {"click": [{"ref_id": "turn0fetch3", "id": 17}]}
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:13: * `find`: {"find": [{"ref_id": "turn0fetch3", "pattern": "Annie Case"}]}
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:14: * `screenshot`: {"screenshot": [{"ref_id": "turn1view0", "pageno": 0}, {"ref_id": "turn1view0", "pageno": 3}]}
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:15: * `finance`: {"finance":[{"ticker":"AMD","type":"equity","market":"USA"}]}, {"finance":[{"ticker":"BTC","type":"crypto","market":""}]}
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:16: * `weather`: {"weather":[{"location":"San Francisco, CA"}]}
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:17: * `sports`: {"sports":[{"fn":"standings","league":"nfl"}, {"fn":"schedule","league":"nba","team":"GSW","date_from":"2025-02-24"}]}
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:18: * `time`: {"time":[{"utc_offset":"+03:00"}]}
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:19: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:20: ---
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:21: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:22: ## Usage hints
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:23: To use this tool efficiently:
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:24: * Use multiple commands and queries in one call to get more results faster; e.g. {"search_query": [{"q": "bitcoin news"}], "finance":[{"ticker":"BTC","type":"crypto","market":""}], "find": [{"ref_id": "turn0search0", "pattern": "Annie Case"}, {"ref_id": "turn0search1", "pattern": "John Smith"}]}
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:25: * Use "response_length" to control the number of results returned by this tool, omit it if you intend to pass "short" in
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:26: * Only write required parameters; do not write empty lists or nulls where they could be omitted.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:27: * `search_query` must have length at most 4 in each call. If it has length > 3, response_length must be medium or long
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:28: * If you find yourself in a situation where you accidentally call the `web.run` tool, it's best just to send an empty query: {"search_query": [{"q": ""}]}.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:29: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:30: ---
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:31: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:32: ## Decision boundary
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:33: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:34: If the user makes an explicit request to search the internet, find latest information, look up, etc (or to not do so), you must obey their request.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:35: When you make an assumption, always consider whether it is temporally stable; i.e. whether there's even a small (>10%) chance it has changed. If it is unstable, you must verify with browsing the internet for verification.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:36: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:37: <situations_where_you_must_browse_the_internet>
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:38: Below is a list of scenarios where browsing the internet MUST be used. PAY CLOSE ATTENTION: you MUST browse the internet in these cases. If you're unsure or on the fence, you MUST bias towards browsing the internet.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:39: - The information could have changed recently: for example news; prices; laws; schedules; product specs; sports scores; economic indicators; political/public/company figures (e.g. the question relates to 'the president of country A' or 'the CEO of company B', which might change over time); rules; regulations; standards; software libraries that could be updated; exchange rates; recommendations (i.e., recommendations about various topics or things might be informed by what currently exists / is popular / is safe / is unsafe / is in the zeitgeist / etc.); and many many many more categories -- again, if you're on the fence, you MUST browse the internet!
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:40:   - For news queries, prioritize more recent events, ensuring you compare publish dates and the date that the event happened.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:41: - The user is seeking recommendations that could lead them to spend substantial time or money -- researching products, restaurants, travel plans, etc.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:42: - The user wants (or would benefit from) direct quotes, links, or precise source attribution.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:43: - A specific page, paper, dataset, PDF, or site is referenced and you haven't been given its contents.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:44: - You're unsure about a fact, the topic is niche or emerging, or you suspect there's at least a 10% chance you will incorrectly recall it
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:45: - High-stakes accuracy matters (medical, legal, financial guidance). For these you generally should search by default because this information is highly temporally unstable
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:46: - The user explicitly says to search, browse, verify, or look it up.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:47: </situations_where_you_must_browse_the_internet>
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:48: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:49: ---
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:50: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:51: ## Citations
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:52: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:53: Results from `web.run` include internal reference IDs such as `turn2search5`. Use
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:54: those reference IDs only in calls to `web.run`; do not expose them in the final
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:55: response.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:56: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:57: Cite sources in the final response using Markdown links:
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:58: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:59: - Cite a single source as `[descriptive source title](https://example.com/page)`.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:60: - Cite multiple sources with separate Markdown links, for example
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:61:   `[first source](https://example.com/one), [second source](https://example.com/two)`.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:62: - Link directly to the page that supports the claim. Do not link to search result
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:63:   pages or use bare URLs.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:64: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:65: Formatting of citations:
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:66: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:67: - Place each citation as near as possible to the claim it supports, normally at
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:68:   the end of the sentence or paragraph and after punctuation.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:69: - Do not place citations inside code fences.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:70: - Do not put citations on a line by themselves or collect all citations at the
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:71:   end of the response.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:72: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:73: If you browse the internet, cite statements supported by web sources. Each cited
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:74: source must directly support the associated claim. Prefer primary and
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:75: authoritative sources, and use sources from different domains when the response
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:76: benefits from multiple perspectives.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:77: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:78: ---
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:79: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:80: ## Special cases
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:81: If these conflict with any other instructions, these should take precedence.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:82: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:83: <special_cases>
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:84: - When the user asks for information about how to use OpenAI products, (ChatGPT, the OpenAI API, etc.), you should check the code in local env and only browse as fallback, when you browse restrict your sources to official OpenAI websites using the domains filter, unless otherwise requested.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:85: - When using search to answer technical questions, you must only rely on primary sources (research papers, official documentation, etc.)
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:86: - Clearly indicate when you are making an inference from sources.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:87: </special_cases>
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:88: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:89: ---
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:90: 
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:91: ## Word limits
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:92: Responses may not excessively quote or draw on a specific source. There are several limits here:
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:93: - **Limit on verbatim quotes:**
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:94:   - You may not quote more than 25 words verbatim from any single non-lyrical source, unless the source is reddit.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:95:   - For song lyrics, verbatim quotes must be limited to at most 10 words.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:96:   - Long quotes from reddit are allowed, as long as you indicate that those are direct quotes via a markdown blockquote starting with ">", copy verbatim, and link the source.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:97: - **Word limits:**
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:98:   - Each webpage source in the sources has a word limit label formatted like "[wordlim N]", in which N is the maximum number of words in the whole response that are attributed to that source. If omitted, the word limit is 200 words.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:99:   - Non-contiguous words derived from a given source must be counted to the word limit.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:100:   - The summarization limit N is a maximum for each source.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:101:   - When using multiple sources, their summarization limits add together. However, each article used must be relevant to the response.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:102: - **Copyright compliance:**
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:103:   - You must avoid providing full articles, long verbatim passages, or extensive direct quotes due to copyright concerns.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:104:   - If the user asked for a verbatim quote, the response should provide a short compliant excerpt and then answer with paraphrases and summaries.
ext/web-search/web_run_description.md:105:   - Again, this limit does not apply to reddit content, as long as it's appropriately indicated that those are direct quotes and you link to the source.

tools/src/catalog_tool_message.rs:17:         tool.description.clone_from(description);
tools/src/catalog_tool_message.rs:18:     }
tools/src/catalog_tool_message.rs:19:     if let Some(parameters) = &messages.parameters {
tools/src/catalog_tool_message.rs:20:         let parameters = parse_catalog_tool_parameters(parameters).and_then(|mut parameters| {
tools/src/catalog_tool_message.rs:21:             if let Some(properties) = &tool.parameters.properties {
tools/src/catalog_tool_message.rs:22:                 for (name, bundled_property) in properties.iter() {
tools/src/catalog_tool_message.rs:23:                     if bundled_property.encrypted == Some(true) {
tools/src/catalog_tool_message.rs:24:                         let property = parameters

tools/src/catalog_tool_message.rs:29:                         property.encrypted = Some(true);
tools/src/catalog_tool_message.rs:30:                     }
tools/src/catalog_tool_message.rs:31:                 }
tools/src/catalog_tool_message.rs:32:             }
tools/src/catalog_tool_message.rs:33:             Ok(parameters)
tools/src/catalog_tool_message.rs:34:         });

tools/src/catalog_tool_message.rs:37:             Err(reason) => tracing::warn!(

tools/src/catalog_tool_message.rs:39:                 reason,
tools/src/catalog_tool_message.rs:40:                 "Invalid catalog tool parameters; using bundled parameters"
tools/src/catalog_tool_message.rs:41:             ),
tools/src/catalog_tool_message.rs:42:         }
